<a id="org65ca555"></a>

### opinions about eradication of some defilements

-   issā and macchariya eradicated in sotāpatti magga
-   some suttas seem to say they may be present in ariyas
-   mātikās: issā and macchariya are eradicated by anāgāmimagga
-   makkha and palāsa: itivuttaka: anāgāmimagga but other places sotāpattimagga
-   kāmacchanda:
    -   atthasalini: anāgāmimagga (considered gross kāmacchanda)
    -   satsatipaṭṭhāna-c: arahattamagga (even subtle kāmacchanda)
-   kukkucca: also opinions
-   other notes
    -   **anāgatavisesa**


<a id="orgc831942"></a>

## 9. Dassanenapahātabbahetukāttika


<a id="org2c5ec69"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **dassanena pahātabbahetukā dhammā**
-   **bhāvanāya pahātabbahetukā dhammā**
-   **nevadassanena na bhāvanāya pahātabbahetukā dhammā**


<a id="org6efcdb0"></a>

### meaning

[

-   **dassanena pahātabba hetuka dhammā**: non-self realities which associate hetus that are to be eradicated by sotāpattimagga
-   **bhāvanāya…**: same for the 3 higher maggas
-   **neva…**: arguments about the meaning &#x2013; whatever is not included in the other 2

] 


<a id="org5a7a45f"></a>

<a id="org5f2ede3"></a>

### acc. to comm.

-   because of issā and macchariya
-   said to be eradicated by sotāpattimagga in comm.
-   but this tika puts them in bhāvanāya
-   issa and macchariya eradicated in 1st magga is apāyagāmini
-   subtle issā macchariya by anāgāmimagga
-   in suttas: AN dukanipāta: pairs of dhammas that could lead to the declining of a sekkha: makkha palasa thamba saramha issa macchariya… implies issā and macchariya is found in lower ariyas
    -   to avoid the problem: sekkha has 2 meanings:
        -   below arahattaphala
        -   sekkha: kalyanaputhujjana tyring to attain as soon as possible
    -   comm.: says it is 7 noble beings
-   duka mātikās: no issā in no-saṁyojana group, so all issā is saṁyojana;
-   asaHkhārika as sas. nature of mohamulacitta: one comm.: neither; another: asaṅkhārika;
    -   if neither, vibhaṅga: 2 moha: asa. and sas. so there should be a neva sa. na sas.
-   gross issā and macchariya and subtle is better division (agrees with tikamatika)
-   abidhamma method: any lobha that is not rupa or arupa is kāmarāga; no lobha that is no saṁyojana
    suttanta: kāmarāga eradicated by anāgāmi
    -   then there is lobha that is nikanti for vipassanā, for example, or own khandhas (kmv), asmimāna, etc.: how to classify it? abhidhamma: kāmarāga; so the eradicated by angm is gross.
-   is issa and macch. exist in ariya? even good puthujjanas dont have it
    -   vigatamaccheramalaṁ: only gross?
    -   2 sotāpanna: one gets something bad, he may feel upset about what he got, but not jelous of what another got
-   conclusion: no dhamma macchariya about lokuttaradhammas
    -   it doesn't mention about pariyatti dhamma macchariya
        -   bhante: thinks they dont have it because it is lower than lokuttaradhamma

<a id="org4bd00dc"></a>

## 10. Ācayagāmittika


<a id="org402ea75"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **ācayagāmino dhammā**
-   **apacayagāmino dhammā**
-   **nevācayagāmināpacayagāmino dhammā**


<a id="orged09069"></a>

### meaning

-   **gāmī**: leading to, going to, heading to
-   **ācayagāmino**: that which leads to accumulation, new existence in saṁsāra &#x2013; lok ku ct and aku ct
-   **apacayagāmino**: leading away from saṁsāra &#x2013; 4 magga cittas
-   **neva.**: neither


<a id="org6262244"></a>

<a id="orgcab66e8"></a>

## 11. Sekkhattika


<a id="org1790bb0"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **sekkhā dhammā**
-   **asekkhā dhammā**
-   **nevasekkhanāsekkhā dhammā**


<a id="org8418f5f"></a>

### meaning

-   **sekkhā dhammā**: 1st 7 loku ctcsk
-   **asekkhā**: arahattaphala
-   **nevasekkhanāsekkhā**: neither


<a id="orgb988936"></a>

<a id="org1b9a2d5"></a>

## 12. Parittattika


<a id="orgccf6491"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **parittā dhammā**
-   **mahaggatā dhammā**
-   **appamāṇā dhammā**


<a id="org698c3eb"></a>

### meaning

-   **paritta**: possess lower capacity, are limited &#x2013; kāmāvacaradhammas
-   **mahaggata**: high &#x2013; rūpāvacara and arūpāvacara dhammas
-   **appamāṇā**: beyond the scope of defilements &#x2013; lokuttaradhammas


<a id="org88e68bf"></a>

<a id="org543f584"></a>

## 13. Parittārammaṇattika


<a id="org1d4da34"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **parittārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **mahaggatārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **appamāṇārammaṇā dhammā**


<a id="org528f124"></a>

### meaning

-   **parittārammaṇa**: dhammas that take parittadhammas as object
-   **mahaggatārammaṇa**: " mahaggatā dhammas
-   **appamāṇārammaṇa**: " appamāṇā dhammas
-   **tikamuttaka**
-   (*:q: why ārammaṇa, not ārammaṇika as in the abdmtsgh?*)


<a id="org158efb1"></a>

### main classification

<td class="org-left">15 rū,3 ākā,3 āki</td>

-   "when": when they take that respective object
-   virati
    -   one does not restrain from a paññatti
    -   one restrains from an action prohibited by a sikkhāpada, not the sikkhāpada itself
    -   when one does not abstain from stealing a table, the rūpas are the object, not the paññatti of table
    -   no vrt with appamāṇa object; one doesn't abstain from lokuttara
    -   ekanta paramatthārammaṇa (no paññatti as object)
        -   8 mku (s, s)
            -   ekanta parittārammaṇa
        -   8 loku (a, t)
            -   ekantappamāṇārammaṇa (Nib)
        -   note: 2 abstentions
            -   abstention from the object
            -   abstention from the action
-   appamañña: ekanta tikamuttaka because it only takes paññatti as object
    -   don't confuse appamaññā with appamāṇa

]

<a id="org1e565c5"></a>

## 14. Hīnattika


<a id="orgf4c0444"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **hīnā dhammā.**
-   **majjhimā dhammā.**
-   **paṇītā dhammā.**


<a id="org09eac75"></a>

### meaning

-   **hīnā dhammā**: akusala
-   **majjhimā dhammā**: other lokiya dhammas
-   **paṇītā dhammā**: lokuttara


<a id="org7df7ed4"></a>

### main classification

[

<table border="2" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="6" rules="groups" frame="hsides">


<colgroup>
<col  class="org-left" />

<col  class="org-left" />

<col  class="org-left" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th scope="col" class="org-left">hīna</th>
<th scope="col" class="org-left">majjhima</th>
<th scope="col" class="org-left">paṇīta</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="org-left">12 aku ct</td>

<a id="orgff24473"></a>

## 15. Micchattattika


<a id="org5c2059a"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **micchattaniyatā dhammā**
-   **sammattaniyatā dhammā**
-   **aniyatā dhammā**


<a id="orgbfd69ff"></a>

### meaning

-   **micchattaniyatā dhammā**: 
    -   micchattattike ‘hitasukhāvahā me bhavissantī’ti evaṃ āsīsitāpi tathā abhāvato, ‘asubhādīsuyeva subha’ntiādi viparītappavattito ca micchāsabhāvāti micchattā; vipākadāne sati khandhabhedānantarameva vipākadānato niyatā; micchattā ca te niyatā cāti micchattaniyatā
        -   in the micchattattika, even though these are wished for with the thought 'these will bring me welfare and happiness,' they are not so, and because they arise with perverse views like 'impure things are pure,' they are of a wrong nature, hence micchattā; when they give results, they are niyatā (fixed) because they give results immediately after the dissolution of the aggregates. since they are wrong and fixed, they are micchattaniyatā
-   **sammattaniyatā dhammā**: that which definitely leads to
    -   vuttaviparītena atthena sammāsabhāvāti sammattā; sammattā ca te niyatā ca anantarameva phaladānenāti sammattaniyatā.
        -   by the opposite meaning of what was stated, they are of a right nature, hence sammattā; and because they are right and fixed by giving results immediately, they are sammattaniyatā
    -   sammatta (sammā tta): right state, rightness, goodness
        -   (*:q: why not ditthadhammavedaniya with special conditions?*)
-   **aniyatā dhammā**: ubhayathāpi na niyatāti aniyatā. Those that are not fixed in either way are aniyatā
-   note:
    -   micchattaniyatā: gives result immediately after death
    -   sammattaniyatā: gives result immediately in the next mind moment
    -   niyatā dhammā: micchattaniyatā and sammattaniyatā


<a id="orge3ecba8"></a>

### notes

<a id="org44f8cea"></a>

## 16. Maggārammaṇattika


<a id="orge904faf"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   **maggārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **maggahetukā dhammā**
-   **maggādhipatino dhammā**


<a id="org3cfe973"></a>

### meaning

[

-   **magga**: lokuttara magga
-   **maggārammaṇā dhammā**: realities that can take lokuttara magga as object (entire cittuppāda)
    -   shows magga cannot be seen by akusala
    -   to eradicate a certain akusala, the mind must go beyond that akusala's scope
        -   jhāna can only suppress kāmataṇhā, not rūpa- arūpa- taṇhā
            -   2nd jhāna can suppress taṇhā for the 1st jhāna, etc
-   **maggahetukā dhammā**: realities that associate maggahetus
    -   -ka: sambandha, connected to maggahetu
    -   3 meanings:
        -   1: realities that associate sampāpakahetus: 8 maggaṅgas taken as a unit
            -   each maggaṅga has the sampāpaka force, but they act as a unity
            -   jhānaṅgas, maggaṅgas, and bojjhaṅgas act as a unit
        -   2: realities that associate mūlahetus in magga: alobha, adosa, and amoha
        -   3: realities that associate sampāpaka and mūlahetu: sammādiṭṭhi
        -   note:
            -   alobha and adosa are mūla but not sampāpaka
            -   amoha is both mūla and sampāpaka
    -   hetu: here, sampāpaka- and mūla- hetus
        -   janakahetu
            -   does not include pacchājātapaccaya
            -   upatthambhaka is included
        -   sampāpakahetu: takes to sugati, duggati, Nibbāna, lokuttara magga
            -   boat that takes one to the other shore
            -   maggapaccaya
            -   maggaṅgas are sampāpakahetus
        -   ñāpakahetu: nāmapaññatti that enables one to understand something
        -   mūlahetu: makes the mind well established on things
            -   vaṭṭamūla: avijjā and taṇhā
                -   comm: can also be anusayas, appahīnakkilesas
    -   notes:
        -   sampāpaka is the main hetu here
        -   related to dassanena pahātabbahetukā dhammā
        -   in paṭṭhāna, each jhānaṅga can be called jhāna; same for maggaṅgas
-   **maggādhipatino dhammā**: dhammas that
    -   1. have magga as a predominant object (ārammaṇādhipati)
        -   abhi and ma do not respect it
            -   abhi: for being lofty or not understanding it
            -   ma: for not understanding it
    -   2. associate adhipati + maggaṅga factors (sahajātādhipati)
    -   3. are adhipati + maggaṅga factors (sahajātādhipati)
    -   2 adhipati
        -   ārammaṇādhipati: respecting magga as an object
            -   only one's own highest magga
                -   illustrates oneself as the most important person for oneself
                -   parents finding out the child is not theirs: love is challenged; love goes away after death; "where is my real child?"; love here depends on "mine"
                -   arahat; even without self idea, respects more his own arahattamagga than even the Buddha's
        -   sahajāta adhipati: being / associating adhipati factors in magga
    -   maggasahajātādhipatino dhammā
        -   dhammas that associate dhammas that are adhipati + maggaṅga
        -   dhammas that are adhipati + maggaṅga: vīriya / vīmaṁsa
    
    -   notes:
        -   respect here is considering it precious, onerous, and putting it above oneself
        -   abhiññā cittas: do not respect magga for being themselves lofty; they are the highest in the ordinary spectrum of cittas; of due to not fully understanding it and Nibbāna
        -   Ma: does not respect magga: like a person who doesn't respect the king
        -   4 adhipati: chanda, vīriya, citta, vīmamsā
            -   only one acts as adhipati at a time
            -   when one is adhipati, the remaining are maggadhipati
            -   vīriya and vīmaṁsā are maggaṅgas
            -   mūla-ṭ: adds a 3rd alternative based on paṭṭhāna
        -   notes:
            -   final outcome of samādhi is abhiññāṇa
            -   final outcome of paññā is arahattamagga
            -   rūpagaruka: people who value rūpa the most

]

1.  cittas that only appear in the Buddhasāsana

    -   8 lokuttara, 8 mki, nirodhasamāpatti, yamakapāṭihāriya, sabbaññutañāṇa, vipassanā cittas, etc.
    -   note: they occur in paccekabuddhas but are not known by the world as in the buddhasāsana


<a id="orgd363cd4"></a>

### main classification

[

-   **maggārammaṇā dhammā**

        <td class="org-left">ñs mku</td>

        -   ekanta tikamuttaka
            -   12 aku
            -   10 viñ
            -   3 manodhātu
            -   11 Td
            -   Hs
            -   ñv mku
            -   ñv mki
            -   15 rpv (-2 abhi)
            -   12 arpv
            -   4 phala

-   notes
    -   phalacitta is not maggahetuka; shows it is not the same citta, though it is immediately after it
    -   Go: puthujjana, but another classification gives it as neither puthujjana nor ariya
    -   occasions magga can be taken as object by lokiyacittas
        -   paccavekkhaṇañāṇa (vimuttiñāṇadassanavisuddhi)
            -   khīṇā jāti vusitaṁ brahmacariyaṁ kataṁ karanīyaṁ nāparaṁ itthattāya
            -   2 types
                -   closely observing
                -   ordinary recollection (not included here)
        -   abhiññā: paracittavijānana, pubbenivāsānussati, anāgataṁsañāṇa
        -   (*:q: are there other occasions?*)
    -   abhi does not respect another reality like kmv
        -   king is respected by everyone but not by mother and father
        -   mahaggatacittas do not "respect" any other reality for being in a higher position
        -   not even Nibbāna
        -   mul-ṭ: abhi cannot take Nib
        -   comm: can take Nib
    -   loku ct has respect for Nib
    -   Ma: do not respect maggact, like mentally disabled people do not respect the king
    -   higher ariya does not respect lower magga ct or other's
    -   cannot forget: ordination, sotapanna, arahatship

]

<a id="org8101a90"></a>

## 17. Uppannattika

1.  Pāḷi

    -   **uppannā dhammā**
    -   **anuppannā dhammā**
    -   **uppādino dhammā**
    
    Uppannattike uppādato paṭṭhāya yāva bhaṅgā uddhaṃ pannā gatā pavattāti uppannā.
    In the Uppannattika, those Dhammas that have arisen, proceeded, and exist from their arising until their dissolution are called uppannā.
    Na uppannāti anuppannā.
    Those that have not arisen are called anuppannā.
    Pariniṭṭhitakāraṇekadesattā avassaṃ uppajjissantīti uppādino.
    Because a part of their cause is completed, they will certainly arise, hence uppādino.

2.  meaning

    -   **uppannā dhammā**: paccuppanna dhammas
    -   **anuppannā dhammā**: anāgata non-vipāka dhammas
    -   **uppādino dhammā**: anāgata vipākadhammas set to arise
    -   (*:q: why aren't atīta dhammas in tikamuttaka?*)
    
    1.  notes
    
        -   uppanna: in some places can include past realities, but not here
        -   anuppanna and uppādino both refer to future realities
        
        1.  anuppanna vs. uppādino vs. anāgata
        
            -   similarity: all not yet arisen
            -   difference:
                -   anuppanna: no vpk ctcsk and kmjr set to arise due to okāsakatakamma
                -   uppādino: only vpk cittacetasika and kmjr set to arise due to okāsakatakamma
                -   anāgata: includes all not-yet-arisen dhammas
        
        2.  uppanna vs. paccuppanna
        
            both are the same

<a id="org391b417"></a>

## 18. Atītattika


<a id="orga2a02d4"></a>

### Pāḷi

-   atītā dhammā
-   anāgatā dhammā
-   paccuppannā dhammā


<a id="org1d7bffc"></a>

### meaning

[

-   **atītā**: arisen and passed away
-   **anāgata**: not yet arisen yet
-   **paccuppanna**: arisen but not passed away yet

] 


<a id="orge4a6e1c"></a>

### main classification

<a id="org9533d0c"></a>

## 19. Atītārammaṇattika


<a id="org689b4d2"></a>

### Pāḷi

[

-   **atītārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **anāgatārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **paccuppannārammaṇa dhammā**

]  


<a id="orgcd9a2e0"></a>

### meaning

-   **atītārammaṇa**: reality that takes a past reality as object
-   **anāgatārammaṇa**: reality that takes a future reality as object
-   **paccuppannārammaṇā**: reality that takes a present reality as object


<a id="orgb55ba70"></a>

-   "when": when they take that respective object
-   ap: ekanta tikamuttaka
-   vrt
    -   sampatta: abstaining from the kmv present object
    -   samādāna: observing precepts
        -   could take present or future objects (abstaining from killing for a period of time; from killing beings born in the future)
    -   samuccheda: magga
    -   paṭipassaddhi: phala
    -   note: always paramatthārammaṇa
        -   takes Nibbāna, but only in lokuttara cittas
    -   abstain from jīvitindriya or the action of killing or the viññattis
    -   2 mm added, not known why (*:q: clarify*)
    -   can take ajjhatta and bahiddha
    -   one book:
        -   killing: dosa mula and moha mula ?
        -   sexual misconduct: also added moha mula
        -   based on the ancient burmese nissaya books
        -
-   kkc: in prtdpn, it is addressed
-   abhi: can take Nibbāna
-   anāgataṁsañāṇa: not separated here but takes future always
    -   (*:q: what about paññātti*)
-   mchry: with atīta object: (harder to understand) other took benefits of your past property; one doesn't like others taking what was already taken
    -   result of kadariyatā, balava macchariya: no one will remember to share merits with you &#x2013; na tesaṁ koci sarati sattānaṁ kammapaccayā
    -   sometimes seems close to issā
-   prmdpn:
    -   kkc: always atītārammaṇa

]

<a id="org5f9e40c"></a>

## 20. Ajjhattattika


<a id="org2fd3ff8"></a>

### Pāḷi

[

-   **ajjhattā dhammā**: internal, belonging to one's own santāna (personal perspective)
    -   ajjhattika/bāhita is universal categorization, but not ajjhatta / bahiddha
    -   only applies to living beings: there is no ajjhatta to the tree
        -   alagaddūpamasutta: cakkhu should not be considered as one's own in vipassanā perspective
-   **bahiddhā dhammā**: external, not belonging to one's own continuum &#x2013; of other beings or inanimate objects
    -   pare: in terms of 4 NT, all saṅkhāras are pare
        -   vipassanā is based on ariyasacca (sukha is dukkha) (*:q: analyse deeper; e.g.: taṇhā is not dukkha in ariyasacca but in vipassanā it is contemplated as so*)
    -   ekantabahiddhā: inanimate realities, always external to all beings
    -   anekantabahiddhā: animate realities that are for that individual internal and for others external
-   **ajjhattabahiddhā dhammā**: can be both internal and external &#x2013;
-   notes:
    -   panpsychism: everything has consciousness, including particles, etc.
        -   in japan: this idea is part of the culture
    -   indriyabaddha: connected with indriyas, especially jīvitindriya (animate)
    -   anindriyabaddha: not so
    -   living does not mean it is atta
    -   upanishad: everything is the same atman, ajjhatta; various levels; brahman, 3 devas part of brahma (shiva, vishnu, krishna); no ajjhattabahiddha; ekattanaya
    -   santāna: must have at least nāma or rūpa; produced by kamma; upapattibhāva, result of kamma, vipāka
    -   **santāna vs santati**
        -   santati: generation, temporal, related to time, always connected with the successive realties, continuation, the continuation of nr process
            -   is a santāna as well
        -   santāna: interrelated, based on the relationship; in a given moment, it is the santāna
            -   nr which act wināmarūpa each other
            -   mind and body are connected; which also shows the ajjhattabahiddhā nature
    -   vipassana: based on paramattha but from ariyasacca perspective
        -   all is pare (under 40 tos)
    -   rūpa body parts that do not contain kammajarūpa can be considered anupādiṇṇaka
        -   anupādiṇṇaka touches anupādiṇṇaka: still pārājika (see methunadhammasikkhāpada)
    -   food, when consumed, becomes ajjhatta
    -   kamma acts on the entire nāmarūpa puñja within the field of the body
    -   a worm in the body is not ajjhatta
    -   a baby is not ajjhatta
    -   artificial things like teeth: not discussed
    -   nissaggiya food turns into the body: not nissaggiya
    -   nissaggiya iron rod in the body
        -   bhante: becomes internal, not nissaggiya because undigested food is also part of the body
    -   manomaya body: bahiddha

]   


<a id="org262568e"></a>

### meaning

1.  Ajjhattattike ‘evaṃ pavattamānā mayaṃ attā’ti gahaṇaṃ, ‘gamissāmā’ti iminā viya adhippāyena attānaṃ adhikāraṃ katvā pavattāti ajjhattā.
2.  In the Ajjhattattika (Intra-personal Triad), the dhammas are called ajjhattā because they arise by taking oneself as the foundation, with the intention, "We who are thus existing shall come to be grasped as self."

‘Ajjhatta’-saddo panāyaṃ gocarajjhatte niyakajjhatte ajjhattajjhatte visayajjhatteti catūsu atthesu dissati.
However, this word ‘ajjhatta’ is seen in four meanings: object-ajjhatta, personal-ajjhatta, intra-ajjhatta, and domain-ajjhatta.
‘‘Tenānanda, bhikkhunā tasmiṃyeva purimasmiṃ samādhinimitte ajjhattameva cittaṃ saṇṭhapetabbaṃ’’ (ma. ni. 3.188), ‘‘ajjhattarato samāhito’’tiādīsu (dha. pa. 362) hi ayaṃ gocarajjhatte dissati.
For example, in passages like: ‘‘Therefore, Ananda, a bhikkhu should establish his mind solely on that former sign of concentration’’ and ‘‘delighting inwardly, concentrated,’’ it is seen in the sense of object-ajjhatta.
‘‘Ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ’’ (dī. ni. 1.228; dha. sa. 161), ‘‘ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharatī’’tiādīsu (dī. ni. 2.373) niyakajjhatte.
In passages like: ‘‘inward clarity’’ and ‘‘or he dwells contemplating dhammas in himself,’’ it is seen in the sense of personal-ajjhatta.
‘‘Cha ajjhattikāni āyatanānī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.304) ajjhattajjhatte.
In passages like: ‘‘six internal sense-bases,’’ it is seen in the sense of intra-ajjhatta.
‘‘Ayaṃ kho panānanda, vihāro tathāgatena abhisambuddho yadidaṃ sabbanimittānaṃ amanasikārā ajjhattaṃ suññataṃ upasampajja viharatī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.187) visayajjhatte; issariyaṭṭhāneti attho.
In passages like: ‘‘Ananda, this dwelling place attained by the Tathagata, is one where, by not attending to any signs, one dwells having entered upon internal emptiness,’’ it is seen in the sense of domain-ajjhatta, meaning a state of mastery.
Phalasamāpatti hi buddhānaṃ issariyaṭṭhānaṃ nāma.
For Buddhas, the attainment of fruits (phalasamāpatti) is indeed a state of mastery.
Idha pana niyakajjhatte adhippeto.
Here, however, personal-ajjhatta is intended.
Tasmā attano santāne pavattā pāṭipuggalikā dhammā ajjhattāti veditabbā.
Therefore, the individual dhammas occurring in one's own continuity should be understood as ajjhattā.

Tato bāhirabhūtā pana indriyabaddhā vā anindriyabaddhā vā bahiddhā nāma.
Those external to oneself, whether bound to faculties or not bound to faculties, are called bahiddhā.
Tatiyapadaṃ tadubhayavasena vuttaṃ.
The third term is stated based on both of these.

Tatiyapadaṃ tadubhayavasena vuttaṃ.
The third term is stated based on both of these.

1.  Anantarattiko teyeva tippakārepi dhamme ārammaṇaṃ katvā pavattanavasena vutto.

<a id="orge95fbc3"></a>

### note

personal perspective here
ajjhattabahiddha: not from an individual perspective; realities which can be ajjhatta and bahiddha at the same time; changes perspective, not individual; inanimate rupa always bahiddha; ajjjhattabahiddha only animate nama rupa; niB always bahiddha

why no paññatti here: ār. tika we classify the citta; not the paññatti itself; paññatti only object;
paññatti bahiddha
comm: not ajjhatta, so put as bahiddha; but actually neither
natthibhāvapñt:

-   of ajjhatta
    -   not bahiddha
    -   doees not seem to apply to the absence of any ajjhatta dhamma
        -   seems so
    -
-   of bahiddha
    -   seems to be bahiddha
    -   only absence of ākā is taken here as neither ajjh nor bahi
    -   not all absenes are so
    -

some tikas put same dhammas in multiple padas


<a id="orgc3055f5"></a>

## 21. Ajjhattārammaṇattika


<a id="org392cad1"></a>

### Pāḷi

[

-   **ajjhattārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **bahiddhārammaṇā dhammā**
-   **ajjhattabahiddhārammaṇā dhammā**

]


<a id="org1c88914"></a>

### meaning

[

-   **ajjhattārammaṇā dhammā**: dhammas that take ajjhattā dhammā as object
-   **bahiddhārammaṇā dhammā**: dhammas that take bahiddhā dhammā as object
-   **ajjhattabahiddhārammaṇā dhammā**: dhammas that take both ajjhattā and bahiddhā dhammā as object

dhsgn-a: Anantarattiko teyeva tippakārepi dhamme ārammaṇaṃ katvā pavattanavasena vutto.
The Anantarattika (Immediate Triad) is stated in terms of occurring by taking those very three kinds of dhammas as objects.
]


<a id="org2dd48cd"></a>

-   note"
    -   "when": when they take that respective object
    -   object of tikamuttaka is natthibhāvapaññatti (absence of internal past ākā)
        -   (*:q: clarify is,mch as anekanta tikamuttaka*)
    -   issā: only bahiddhārammaṇa
    -   macchariya: here, only ajjhattār.
        -   some: should be put as bahiddhār. as well
        -   here, ajjhatta includes what considered as "mine"
            -   but kadariyatā takes even beyond what is "mine", so macchariya should not be classified in this way
    -   virati: no paññattārammaṇa
    -   appamañña: only bahiddhārammaṇa
    -   pñt:
        -   normally: neither ajjh nor bahiddha
        -   here: bahiddha since it is not ajjhatta
        -   natthibhāvapaññatti
            -   normal nothingness: bahiddha
            -   nothingness of a bahiddha reality: bahiddha
            -   nothingness of an ajjhatta reality: neither ajjhatta nor bahiddha
                -   only applied to the obj of the āki (nothingness of ākā)
                -   it is not clear why only this obj is classified so
    -   (*:q: can have issa for paññatti? for paṭibhāganimitta?*)
    -   macchariya, yes
    -   issā, yes
    -   if mach is not only ajjhattār., possible to have mch for this natthibhāvapaññatti; issā for someone else's natthubhāvapaññatti also seems possible; this amount to the absence of bahiddha object, so it seems bahiddha; there seems to be the possibility for issā to arise
    -   another issue
    -   iṭṭhār; kusalavipāka vpk
    -   in manodv vīthi; obj is lobhamūlacitta / dmm / mmc / cv ; ku or aku vpk Td?
    -   what about nāmadhammas as iṭṭha/aniṭṭha?
    -   (*:q: investigate: absence of a reality as a paññatti and as cessation of a reality as nibbāna*)
    -   3 āki
        -   absence of a reality is not constructed by the mind; absence of an internal reality is not considered as either int or ext
            -   bhante: should be applied to external realities as well
            -   abhāvapaññatti / natthibhāvapaññatti: special nature
                -   dhsgn: separated from other paññattis
        -   paññatti is synthesized by the mind based on vikappa; considered bahiddhā
            -   actually neither ajjhatta nor bahiddha, but here bahiddha bc it's not internal
                -   not absolutely bahiddha
        -   (*:q: what about the limited space kasiṇa; isn't that the absence already? if it is rupa there would be ākā from it*)
        -   (*:q: why is it not external?*)
    -   cetasikas
    -   issā: ekanta bahiddhārammaṇa
        -   (*:q: clarify*)
    -   macchariya: (*:q: clarify why not bahiddhārammaṇa*)
        -   here, it seems that ajjhatta includes what is considered as mine in an ordinary sense, so macchariya only arises with ajjhatta objects
    -   vrt: here, considered to be able to take ajjhatta objects
        -   (*:q: find an example of such occasion*): abstaining from a mental state?
    -   appamañña: (*:q: why not ajjhattārammaṇa such as in karuna for oneself?*)

] 

1.  acc. to comm.

    -   anekanta bahiddhār: 52 csk
    -   ajjh. bahiddhār.: 49 csk (is, ap)
    -   macchariya: ekanta ajjhatta
        -   why? ajjhatta here includes all that is considered mine
        -   jnk: not ekanta ajjhatta
    -   ajjhattār. tika is based on ajjhattattika
    -   kkc: ekanta ajjhattār
        
        notes:
        issa macchariya and kkc leave some debate
        
        dassanatika: changes based on issa and macchariya
        matika: anagamimagga
        comm: sotapattimagga
        
        macchariya: external objects as well
        
        prdpn: kkc only past; chart again changes
        
        these 3 are not scrutinized in the matika tradition

<a id="org0edf140"></a>

# Essay: Teaching Mātikā


<a id="org920c8ca"></a>

## Introduction

The Abhidhammapiṭaka, the basket of higher Dhamma, is one of the three baskets that comprise the Buddha's words in the Pāḷi language and constitute the entirety of the most ancient and orthodox Buddhist tradition, the Theravāda. It is in this basket where the Buddha analyses and elucidates with precision the very foundations of living beings, the world at large, and its inner workings.  Equipped with this knowledge of the Abhidhamma, the Noble seeker is able to grasp the deep significance of the Buddha's words, clarify them to others, and apply this unparalelled clarity to the meditative endeavour, facilitating the breakthrough to Nibbāna, the *summum bonum* of Buddhism.

The first book of the Abhidhamma, the "Dhammasaṅgaṇī", lays out profound classifications of realities &#x2013; dhammas &#x2013; from various perspectives by grouping them into triads, such as wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate realities; as well as dyads, such as root-cause and non-root-cause realities.

However, entering into the dense depths of the Abhidhamma, with its own jargon, pithiness, and subtlety, is no easy task.  This led to the creation of compendiums, summaries, and commentaries, such as the Abhidhammatthasaṅgaha, the Compendium of the Abhidhamma, authored by V. Ācariya Anuruddha, which became the traditional entryway to this part of the doctrine.

This essay proposes an alternative pathway through the Abhidhamma doctrine taking its first book, the Dhammasaṅgaṇī, as starting point and attempts to include, in a general manner, all the main doctrinal points in a systematic, beginner-friendly order.  It assumes a general familiarity with the doctrine and its key terms, and merely delineates the order in which topics might be introduced to a student without explaining them.


<a id="orgbc3b4ea"></a>

## Dhamma

Dhamma, a versatile word in the Buddhist doctrine, is the very entrance to this study.  From the start, the philosophical concepts of what realities &#x2013; atthas &#x2013; are and an overview of their classifications into inferior, superior, material, immaterial, conceptual, and so on should be made clear.


<a id="org4eb4c60"></a>

## Wholesome Triad


<a id="org18c2e75"></a>

### Kusalā dhammā

The term kusala &#x2013; wholesome &#x2013; serves as the starting point for classifying such dhammas.  The sense-sphere realms, in particular the so called good destinations, are introduced, their 8 wholesome consciousnesses, and their respective mental factors, with an emphasis on mūlahetus.

The ten basis for making merit &#x2013; puññakiriyavatthus &#x2013; then serve as practical examples of these mental states in action, and bhāvanā, meditation, then introduces the practice of serenity (samathabhāvanā), during which the student begins to practically develop meditation in parallel to the study.   Serenity then leads to the introduction of the fine-material realms and their five wholesome absorption consciousnesses, followed by the immaterial realms and their respective four wholesome immaterial absorptions.

The next step is to introduce vipassanābhāvanā &#x2013; insight meditation &#x2013; leading to the supramundane sphere and its Path consciousnesses, in brief and in detail, including overviews on the topics of the Noble Truth of Cessation and its Path, giving the student a general picture of the entire training and its goal.


<a id="orgf308e90"></a>

### Akusalā dhammā

The Path consciousnesses eradicate defilements, and this links to the unwholesome consciousnesses.  The apāya realms are explained, linking with the Noble Truth of Suffering.  The lust-rooted consciousnesses introduce the topic of the Noble Truth of the Cause of Suffering, as well as other lust-associated mental factors.

Next, the  dosahetu is brought up, together with its anger-rooted mentalities, followed by the mohahetu with its exclusive mental factor of skepticism.


<a id="orgd57fdac"></a>

### Abyākatā dhammā

1.  Vipāka

    After wholesome and unwholesome dhammas, the indeterminate dhammas are introduced, starting with resultant mentalities.  When discussing the ahetukavipākacittas, the concepts of vatthu, viññāṇa, ārammaṇa, and kicca are introduced in order to explain their names and functions.  The eight mahāvipākas, rūpāvacara and arūpāvacara vipākas, their respective realms of existence in greater detail, and finally lokuttara phalas are then explained one by one.

2.  Kiriya

    Next, the functional consciousnesses are explained, starting with the āvajjanacittas, which also introduces dvāras, and hasituppādacitta, with the 6 types of smiles.  The remaining functional consciousnesses are also explained according to realm as well as the lack of functional dhammas in lokuttarabhūmi, completing all consciousnesses and their mental factors.

3.  Mental Processes and Principles

    With the completion of nāmadhammas, further details on their workings are introduced, starting with the types of mental processes as well as the nāmadhammas outside processes, which includes the in-depth analysis of how volition &#x2013; kamma &#x2013; produces results.

4.  Matter

    At this point, the student has finished the doctrinal points directly connected to mind and mental factors and moves to the analysis of matter &#x2013; rūpa&#x2013; which includes the great primary elements, their derived forces, and their combinations into groups.

5.  Nibbāna

    Rūpa concludes all worldly formations, and the student is taken into an in-depth analysis of the Unformed Element of Nibbāna, concluding the explanations connected with the wholesome triad.


<a id="orgb314ac5"></a>

## Feeling and other triads

When entering the vedanāttika, the student is introduced to a deeper understanding of feeling, its classifications according to experience, faculty, etc. and the vedanāsaṅgaha, as well as the classifications of ultimate realities according to the vedanāttika.

The following tikas are then taught with more ease by simply introducing new terminology when needed and their general classifications.

At the conclusion of the main classifications, the student is then brought back to the beginning, and the various saṅgahas are introduced: akusalasaṅgahas, missakasaṅgahas, bodhipakkhiyasaṅgahas, sabbasaṅgahas, etc., including khandhas, āyatanas, dhātus, and saccas, and the student is given the opportunity to review previous concepts and apply the knowledge to the various classifications, solidifying the knowledge and retention.


<a id="org3eb15c3"></a>

## Conditionality

With the conclusion of the saṅgahas, all material and immaterial dhammas have been discussed and classified, and all that remains is the analysis of conditionality &#x2013; how one dhamma serves as a condition for another in various ways.

This begins with Paṭiccasamuppāda &#x2013; Dependent Origination &#x2013; and is then followed by what is considered the pinnacle of the Abhidhamma doctrine &#x2013; the Paṭṭhāna.


<a id="org5088bff"></a>

## Conclusion

This concludes the foundational studies of the Abhidhamma doctrine starting with its first book, the Dhammasaṅgaṇī.

This method blends conceptual descriptions with enumerations and practical meditation, keeping the student engaged, and by adding the Saṅgahas at a later stage, it reiterates the knowledge facilitating retention.


<a id="org2ec34fa"></a>

## Appendix: Overview of the Study Path

This appendix provides a structured outline of the core elements included in this study path:

1.  Dhamma
    -   Meaning and scope of **Dhamma**
    -   Concepts of **Attha**, **Paramattha**, and **Hīnattha**
    -   Fundamental realities: **Citta**, **Cetasika**, **Rūpa**, and **Nibbāna**
2.  Kusalattika
    -   **Kusala**
        -   Meaning of Kusala
        -   **Kusalacittas** and **Cetasikas**
        -   **Kāma Bhūmi**
            -   The 8 **Mahākusalacittas**
                -   **Kusalahetus** and the 4 types of roots
                -   **Sobhanacetasikas**
                -   The 10 **Puññakiriyavatthus**
                    -   Note: Focus on Bhāvanā leading to Kammaṭṭhāna
        -   **Bhāvanā**
            -   **Samathabhāvanā**
                -   **Rūpabhūmi**
                    -   The 5 **Rūpāvacara Kusalacittas** and **Cetasikas**
                -   **Arūpabhūmi** 
                    -   The 4 **Arūpāvacara Kusalacittas** and **Cetasikas**
            -   **Vipassanābhāvanā**
                -   **Lokuttarabhūmi**
                    -   The 4 **Magga Cittas** and **Cetasikas**
                        -   The 8 **Maggaṅgas**
                        -   **Nirodhasacca** and **Maggasacca**
                            -   Link: Maggacittas remove Akusalas
    -   **Akusala**
        -   Meaning of Akusala
        -   **Apāyabhūmi**
            -   **Dukkhasacca**
        -   **Akusalacittas** and **Cetasikas**
            -   Lobhamūlacittas
                -   The 4 **Akusalasādhāraṇa Cetasikas**
                -   **Lōbhādittika**
                    -   **Lobhahetu**
                        -   **Samudayasacca**
                -   **Thīnamiddha**
            -   Dosamūlacittas
                -   **Dosādicatukka**
                -   **Dosahetu**
            -   Mohamūlacittas
                -   **Mohahetu**
                -   **Vicikicchā**
    -   **Abyākata** 
        -   **Vipākacittas** and **Cetasikas**
            -   The 15 **Ahetukavipākas**
                -   Associated concepts: **Vatthu**, **Viññāṇa**, **Ārammaṇa**, and **Kicca** and their saṅgahas
            -   The 8 **Mahāvipākas**
            -   The 9 **Mahaggatavipākas**
            -   The 4 **Phalacittas**
        -   **Kiriyacittas** and **Cetasikas**
            -   **Ahetuka Kiriyas**
                -   Concept of **Dvāra**
            -   The 8 **Mahākiriyas**
            -   The 9 **Mahaggatakiriyas**
        -   **Vīthis**
        -   **Vīthimutta**
            -   Relation to **Kamma**
        -   **Rūpa**
        -   **Nibbāna**
3.  Vedanāttika
    -   Meaning of Vedanā
    -   **Types** of vedanā
    -   **Vedanāsaṅgaha**
    -   Classifications
4.  Other Tikas
    -   Explanation of new terms introduced by these classifications
    -   General Classiication of Realities
5.  Saṅgahas and Comprehensive Classification of Realities
6.  Conditionality (Including **Paṭiccasamuppāda** and **Paṭṭhāna**)
